r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer sequence A = (A_1, A_2, \dots, A_N) of length N.
You can perform the following operation any number of times, possibly zero:

- Choose an integer pair (i, j) satisfying 1 \leq i \lt j \leq N, and replace A_i with A_i + 1 and A_j with A_j - 1.

Determine whether it is possible to make A a non-decreasing sequence through the operations.
You are given T test cases. Solve each of them.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format. Here, \mathrm{case}_i denotes the i-th test case.
T
\mathrm{case}_1
\mathrm{case}_2
\vdots
\mathrm{case}_T

Each test case is given in the following format:
N
A_1 A_2 \dots A_N

Output

Print T lines. The i-th line should contain the answer for the i-th test case.
For each test case, if it is possible to make A a non-decreasing sequence through the operations, print Yes; otherwise, print No.

Constraints


- 1 \leq T \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 2 \leq N \leq 2 \times 10^5
- 0 \leq A_i \leq 10^9
- The sum of N over all test cases is at most 2 \times 10^5.
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

3
3
1 7 5
2
9 0
10
607 495 419 894 610 636 465 331 925 724

Sample Output 1

Yes
No
Yes

In the first test case, you can make A into a non-decreasing sequence by performing the following operations:

- Choose (i, j) = (1, 2). After the operation, A is (2, 6, 5).
- Choose (i, j) = (1, 2). After the operation, A is (3, 5, 5).

In the second test case, you cannot make A into a non-decreasing sequence no matter how you perform the operations.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

But let's not jump. Need prove sufficiency. There is known result: Given integer sequence A, can transform to nondecreasing by moving units left iff for all k, prefix sum P_k <= k * total / N. This is exactly "A is majorized by uniform distribution"? Let's verify with operation and final nondecreasing. Another approach: Since operation can move mass left, the set of reachable sequences are those B with same sum and prefix sums >= A. We need existence of nondecreasing B in that set. The set of nondecreasing sequences with sum M has prefix sums bounded above by line. The condition that A's prefix sums are below line might be sufficient because the uniform sequence (or near-uniform) is nondecreasing and has prefix sums roughly line, which dominates A if A below line. But uniform sequence may not be integer if M not divisible by N. However we can choose B_i = floor(M/N) or ceil(M/N) sorted nondecreasing: e.g., q repeated N-r and q+1 repeated r, where M = qN + r, 0<=r<N. This sequence is nondecreasing (q...q, q+1...q+1) and sum M. Its prefix sums are:
For k <= N-r: S'_k = k q.
For k > N-r: S'_k = (N-r)q + (k-(N-r))(q+1) = k q + max(0, k-(N-r)).
Is this always >= S_k if S_k <= kM/N? Since S'_k is the smallest? Wait uniform-ish sequence has prefix sums maybe below line for some k? Let's compute M=13,N=3: q=4,r=1, sequence (4,4,5). But let's ensure no hidden issue: Operation choose i<j, A_i+1, A_j-1. We assumed final prefix sums >= initial. Let's verify operation effect on prefix sums precisely:
For i<j, S_k = sum_{1..k} A_t.
- k < i: no A_i or A_j included -> no change.
- i <= k < j: A_i included (+1), A_j not included -> +1.
- k >= j: both included (+1 and -1) -> no change.
So S_k increases by 1 for k in [i, j-1]. Thus prefix sums nondecreasing. Conversely, any interval increment [i,j-1] corresponds to operation (i,j). Good.

Implementation:
def solve():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    T = int(input())
    out = []
    for _ in range(T):
        N = int(input())
        A = list(map(int, input().split()))
        M = sum(A)
        q, r = divmod(M, N)
        # N-r is count of q's
        cnt_q = N - r
        S = 0
        ok = True
        for k in range(1, N):  # k from 1 to N-1
            S += A[k-1]
            U = k * q
            if k > cnt_q:
                U += k - cnt_q
            if S > U:
                ok = False
                # Cannot break? We can break to save time but need consume input already consumed. A already read. We can break loop and output No.
                # But if break, no need further S. Fine.
                break
        # We don't need check k=N because S_N=M, U_N=M.
        out.append("Yes" if ok else "No")
    print("\n".join(out))

Need ensure if break early, we don't need process remaining A? A list already read, so fine. But if we break, no issue. Could also not break to keep simple. Sum N 2e5, no problem. But if break early, okay. Need maybe if N=2, loop k=1 only. Good.

Implementation details:
def solve():
    import sys
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    idx = 0
    T = data[idx]; idx += 1
    ans = []
    for _ in range(T):
        N = data[idx]; idx += 1
        # We can compute sum and prefix in one pass without storing A? Need A values for prefix loop. We can read slice A = data[idx:idx+N]; idx += N.
        A = data[idx:idx+N]; idx += N
        M = sum(A)
        q, r = divmod(M, N)
        cnt_q = N - r
        S = 0
        ok = True
        for k in range(1, N):
            S += A[k-1]
            U = k * q
            if k > cnt_q:
                U += k - cnt_q
            if S > U:
                ok = False
                break
        ans.append("Yes" if ok else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

Let's write code robustly:
import sys

def solve():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]
    idx = 1
    ans = []
    for _ in range(t):
        n = data[idx]; idx += 1
        A = data[idx:idx+n]; idx += n
        total = sum(A)
        q, r = divmod(total, n)
        cnt_q = n - r
        pref = 0
        ok = True
        for k in range(1, n):
            pref += A[k-1]
            limit = k * q
            if k > cnt_q:
                limit += k - cnt_q
            if pref > limit:
                ok = False
                break
        ans.append("Yes" if ok else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()

total = sum(data[idx:idx+n])? That also slices. We can use loop to sum and prefix in one pass:
pref=0; total=0; ok=True; limit? Need q depends on total, so need total first. We can compute total by loop over data indices, then second loop for prefix. Or store A. Slicing is fine.

But if T=2e5, creating many small lists may be overhead but sum N 2e5, so at most 2e5 list creations of length2? Actually list slicing overhead ~0.1us? 2e5 maybe 0.02s? Fine. But to be safe, we can avoid slicing and compute total and prefix using data indices. Let's design efficient no-slice:

Read all data. For each test:
n = data[idx]; idx += 1
# compute total and maybe store A? We can compute total by summing data[idx:idx+n] but slice. Instead:
total = 0
for j in range(idx, idx+n):
    total += data[j]
# Now compute q,r. Then prefix loop:
pref = 0
ok = True
for k in range(1, n):
    pref += data[idx + k - 1]
    ...
idx += n
This avoids slice and extra memory. Two passes over A per test, total O(sum N). Good. We'll use this.

Need be careful: In prefix loop, data[idx + k - 1] for k=1 gives A_1. After loop, idx += n. If ok becomes False and break, we still need idx += n after loop. Code:
def solve():
    import sys
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]
    idx = 1
    out = []
    for _ in range(t):
        n = data[idx]; idx += 1
        start = idx
        total = 0
        for j in range(start, start+n):
            total += data[j]
        q, r = divmod(total, n)
        cnt_q = n - r
        pref = 0
        ok = True
        for k in range(1, n):
            pref += data[start + k - 1]
            limit = k * q
            if k > cnt_q:
                limit += k - cnt_q
            if pref > limit:
                ok = False
                break
        idx = start + n
        out.append("Yes" if ok else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

This is efficient.

Now, let's plan final answer code:
```python
import sys

def solve():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]
    idx = 1
    ans = []
    for _ in range(t):
        n = data[idx]
        idx += 1
        start = idx
        total = 0
        for p in range(start, start + n):
            total += data[p]
        q, r = divmod(total, n)
        cnt_q = n - r
        pref = 0
        ok = True
        for k in range(1, n):
            pref += data[start + k - 1]
            limit = k * q
            if k > cnt_q:
                limit += k - cnt_q
            if pref > limit:
                ok = False
                break
        idx = start + n
        ans.append("Yes" if ok else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```

import sys

def solve():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]
    idx = 1
    ans = []

        n = data[idx]

        cnt_q = n - r
        pref = 0
        ok = True
        for k in range(1, n):
            pref += data[start + k - 1]
            limit = k * q
            if k > cnt_q:

            if pref > limit:
                ok = False
                break
        idx = start + n
        ans.append("Yes" if ok else "No")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":

Let's maybe formalize the reachable set:
Let P_k = sum_{i=1}^k A_i. Operation (i,j) increases P_k by 1 for i<=k<j. Thus P_k never decreases. Conversely, for any nonnegative integers d_k (1<=k<N), performing operation (k,k+1) d_k times increases P_k by d_k and leaves other prefix sums unchanged. Therefore a sequence B with prefix sums Q_k is reachable iff Q_k >= P_k for all k and Q_N=P_N. This is exact. Then we need Q_k of a nondecreasing sequence. The maximum possible Q_k is U_k. So condition.

This is a nice explanation but final code only.

```
limit = 0
for k in range(1, n):
    pref += data[start+k-1]
    if k <= cnt_q:
        limit += q
    else:

        ok = False
        break
```
This computes U_k incrementally. It might be easier to verify. Let's test with M=12,N=5,q=2,r=2,cnt_q=3:

limit = 0

Code:
```
        limit = 0
        pref = 0
        ok = True
        for k in range(1, n):
            pref += data[start + k - 1]
            if k <= cnt_q:
                limit += q
            else:
                limit += q + 1
            if pref > limit:
                ok = False
                break
```
Note: If r=0, cnt_q=n, so for k=1..n-1, limit += q. U_N would be n*q=M. Good.
If r>0, for k>cnt_q, limit += q+1. After loop k=n-1, limit = U_{n-1}. U_N = U_{n-1} + (q+1 if n>cnt_q else q) = M. Good.

Now, let's think if there is any hidden "A_i" can be 0, so if total M=432,N=3, q
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def solve():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    t = data[0]
    idx = 1
    ans = []

    for _ in range(t):
        n = data[idx]
        idx += 1
        start = idx

        total = 0
        for p in range(start, start + n):
            total += data[p]

        q, r = divmod(total, n)
        cnt_q = n - r  # number of q's in the balanced nondecreasing sequence

        pref = 0
        limit = 0
        ok = True

        for k in range(1, n):
            pref += data[start + k - 1]

            if k <= cnt_q:
                limit += q
            else:
                limit += q + 1

            if pref > limit:
                ok = False
                break

        idx = start + n
        ans.append("Yes" if ok else "No")

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
